ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u337	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour on behalf of the Law and Justice Club to present a position on the proposed Act on Accessibility for people with special needs, print No 3579. The draft Act on Accessibility for people with special needs is the first system solution in Poland. The aim of this project is to gradually improve the availability of public entities so that people with specific needs can use public services as independently as possible. Removing barriers and ensuring accessibility for all citizens, including especially those experiencing mobility or perception difficulties, is one of the key tasks of a country that implements the principle of social justice. Improving social inclusion and creating a society of equal opportunities depend to a large extent on the implementation of accessibility, which as a feature of infrastructure and services ensures their easier use and use. The current regulatory system at both statutory and executive levels does not have satisfactory effects on this accessibility. The rules of law are scattered and insufficiently effective. What has become a standard in Western or Northern European countries in implementing infrastructure investments is still just an example of the goodwill of investors or local authorities interested in improving selected aspects of buildings or their surroundings, which are, however, mostly different in terms of quality and technical. The Polish legal order lacks solutions to define minimum technical requirements to ensure the availability of public buildings. The same is true for public services and communication. As a result of the inspection carried out by the NIK in the information of 30 October 2018, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [2] appointing the members and alternate members of the Committee of the Regions for the period from 30 October 2018 to 30 October 2018. The availability of public space for older people and disabled people has been found to be a general state of accessibility of public space in Poland is definitely unsatisfactory. NIK pointed out that already at the stage of preparation and design, some of the investments lacked solutions to improve the availability of public spaces. In addition, almost 40% cases of design, technical or tender documentation did not take into account solutions for people with different disabilities, and the concepts of improvement mainly concerned people with mobility disabilities without other disabilities or the needs of older people. For example, for a person who has difficulty moving due to advanced age, a disabled person or a parent with a baby carriage, moving around the office or handling an official matter is generally a challenge due to a number of barriers in the form of stairs and the location of customer service places without taking into account the aspect of availability. Turning to the proposed law, we notice a significant change in relation to the legislative solutions already adopted in Poland in the form of a dictionary in which innovative definitions have been formulated using the perspective of the UN Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities. Art. 2 introduces definitions: barriers, accessibility, people with special needs, universal design, rational improvement. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 3 The Act indicates a catalogue of entities collectively named public entities which are obliged to ensure the accessibility of the Act. The Act also requires public entities to publish on the website of the entity information about the scope of their activities in the form of an accessible electronic file, recording of content in Polish sign language, information in a text that is easy to read. The role of the proposed law is not only to identify an obligation for public entities, but also to provide tools for achieving the objectives of this regulation and to create a system framework to ensure accessibility in all public policies. In accordance with Chapter 5 of the draft law, a new special fund of the Accessibility Fund will be created to be managed by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. In conclusion: the implementation of the solutions provided for in the proposed law will accelerate the process of improving accessibility. This increase in accessibility will benefit from [[♪ The bell ♪]] mainly people with disabilities, older people, seniors and all other people suffering from various difficulties, barriers every day. The Law and Justice Club fully supports the proposed law. People with special needs will be able to use public services as independently as possible. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u369	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Dear Izbo! First of all, I would like to thank you very much for your appreciation for the bill. Ladies and gentlemen! I'm gonna try to take it one step at a time. If I miss anything, we'll explain. Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, with regard to the draft law on accessibility for people with special needs, this is not a project that refers to new buildings, but we want to regulate the issue of buildings that already exist and which are not accessible to people with disabilities. As regards the amendment of the Construction Law, we are in the process of amending this document. We're gonna process it, we're gonna change it to take into account accessibility issues. First of all, after the entry into force of the Act on Accessibility to persons with special needs, all resorts will have 12 months to review their legal acts and report any changes to those acts, which will be considered by the Accessibility Council and subsequently notified by the Minister for Regional Development to possible deliberations. With regard to new jobs and extensive administration, we do not plan to create new jobs under the law. As regards the issue of the accessibility coordinator, it may be a person - there is no requirement to create a new job - who is already employed in an institution. It may also be a few people dealing with this as part of additional tasks. As far as certification is concerned, it is not mandatory, ladies and gentlemen. It's not from the bill. Certification is voluntary and applies to private entities where they wish to obtain the benefits referred to in the Accessibility Act. Then they're certified. Certification is to be carried out by entities which have powers to do so, so that it is carried out on the basis of uniform standards and uniform criteria. Today everyone can issue a certificate, a document confirming that an institution is available, but it does so on the basis of its own criteria. We want to have surveillance and know that these buildings are actually available to people with special needs. That is why we want this to be done on the basis of uniform standards. The issue of finance has been raised many times. Ladies and gentlemen, as we have already mentioned, the programme "Availability" is talking about PLN 23 billion. Nobody takes that money and nobody says the program isn't going to happen. A number of initiatives have been identified under the "Availability+" programme, many actions already being carried out, such as the exchange of rolling stock: rail and bus, investments in schools, universities, health care institutions and many other projects. These are mainly pilot projects aimed at showing issues related to improving accessibility. It is already happening, it is implemented within the framework of this 23 billion PLN. These measures shall be engaged within the framework of national operational programmes financed by the European Union. As regards the 375 million PLN indicated in the Act on Accessibility to persons with special needs, these are measures which constitute costs implementation of the law. They will come from the state budget - 90% These appropriations are the Accessibility Fund - and are intended for the next 10 years. Ladies and gentlemen! 23 billion zlotys, which are dedicated to the implementation of the "Availability+' programme, are only the beginning, as many other actions financed by the funds are being implemented. The new financial perspective will include these accessibility issues. I can say that the European Commission is very positive about this type of action and investment, so I think that these measures will certainly be greater. As far as the scale of needs is concerned, it is very large. As far as issues relating to the definition of construction standards are concerned, we will want to develop them. We will certainly consult widely with the environment of people with disabilities, because they are the best source of information in this area. As regards Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego and the Accessibility Fund, the agreement was signed with the bank. The Accessibility Fund will be starting soon, the first competitions will be in September. The Fund will be funded from a wide range of sources: both the Solidarity Fund for the Support of Persons with Disabilities and the State budget, and the EU funds from the repayment of loans from the 2007-2013 perspective. At the moment, it is therefore difficult to estimate the average annual budget of this fund. The Accessibility Fund will finance exclusive investments related to accessibility in competitions announced by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. The first contest to be held in September will be a pilot contest. As part of this competition we predict up to 40% redemptions for residential buildings. As regards loans, they will be more favourable than other loans available on the market. As far as the article is concerned. 7, i.e. technical and legal conditions and alternative access - yes, it will be monitored, firstly, because after 2 years alternative access will have to be sanctioned. Secondly, in the report on accessibility, which each institution will submit to the Minister for Development every 4 years, it will be necessary to indicate the conditions under which alternative access was provided. In addition, you will be able to complain about alternative access if the person who accesses such access is not satisfied. I've already talked about the certification obligation, so I think I've discussed all the issues that have been raised. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u375	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present our position towards the government's draft law amending the Social Assistance Act and the Mental Health Act, print No 3524. The provisions of the draft law cover two areas. The proposed regulations are mostly based on the practice of aid measures showing a number of examples of the need for change. The aim of the proposed law is among others. reduce the scale of irregularities in facilities providing 24-hour care to disabled persons, chronically ill or elderly persons, thereby increasing the safety of elderly and disabled persons in these facilities, improving the effectiveness of sanctions imposed on operators without authorisation, and improving the conditions of residence of persons staying in legally operating 24-hour care facilities. The amendment also aims to facilitate the authorisation of establishments, particularly those with little scope, by relaxing certain standards and clarifying the provisions on the documents required for the application for authorisation. The draft law also regulates the rules of residence in institutions and the rules of leaving them by persons to whom the Law of 19 August 1994 on the protection of mental health applies. This important aspect of the project stresses, on the one hand, the issue of responsible care and safety for people with mental disorders, and on the other, it is intended to ensure that their personal freedoms are respected. It is contrary to the law of these people to contact the outside world and to self-decision about going beyond the premises. The rules of restrictions in this respect should be regulated and the only objective obstacle may be the poor health of those persons confirmed by the relevant medical certificate. In view of the demands of those whose parents have been sent to a social assistance home, the Act will also extend the catalogue of situations where the obligation to pay a social assistance home payment may be exempted. The proposed law also aims to clarify certain provisions of the procedure for granting, granting and paying social assistance benefits. By appropriate provisions, the designer also limited the possibility of obtaining authorisation to operate an establishment by entities which the voivode has imposed in the past at least twice a fine for running an establishment without authorisation or has decided to suspend the operation of the establishment. Those entities will not be allowed to obtain authorisation within 5 years of the punishment. At the same time, the 5-year period provides operators with an opportunity to adapt their activities to the requirements of the Act and obtain authorisation in the future. I would like to stress that the bill was the subject of consultation and opinion, and their comments at the stage of work alongside numerous trade union and non-governmental organisations were also passed on to voivodships and voivodships. The Joint Committee on Government and Local Government expressed a positive opinion on the draft. On 16 July this year, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy gave a unanimous positive opinion on the draft law. The government project is based on the real need to clarify the existing legal provisions and responds to the needs and expectations of the subject-matter of the regulation. In view of the positive effects of the proposed changes, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the government draft law amending the Social Assistance Act and the Law on the Protection of Mental Health. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u399	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I wanted to start by thanking the members of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy. We had a meeting yesterday. This bill received, so to speak, a clear positive assessment, positive recommendations from all members of the committee. I would also like to thank the representatives of all parliamentary clubs for their declaration of support for this bill, but also for the many positive words I have heard from you about the solutions contained in this bill. Indeed, it is a project which is a result of cooperation with practices, with those directly working in the field of social assistance, whose regulation we are trying to modernise and improve in this bill. I will try to address the questions that were asked during the debate on the bill. Mr Szczerba asked about issues related to the control of the proper functioning of homes and social services. I remember that in this Chamber I had the opportunity to present detailed statistical information concerning recent years and how these checks were carried out in 2019. I also have data covering the years 2016 and 2017. As regards the number of checks carried out by the voivodship offices in 2016, there were 237 of them, while 222 checks took place in 2017. With regard to the number of facilities that were sanctioned for conducting them without authorisation, one of the questions for 24-hour care concerned this issue, we have, I stress, 36 facilities in 2016 and 39 facilities in 2019. With regard to the number of facilities that, despite the imposition of the penalty, continued to take care of people with disabilities 24 hours a day, we had 37. Let me remind you that this issue is also covered by the regulation contained in the draft law. It is about strengthening criminal or financial sanctions for institutions which, despite the imposition of sanctions, continue to operate. With regard to the number of establishments that received a decision ordering the cessation of 24-hour care activities, there were four such facilities in 2016 and in 2017 the number was zero. As regards the number of facilities providing 24-hour care for disabled persons, chronically ill or elderly, the number of such facilities in Poland was 434, and this is the November 2017 data. As we can see, about half of these facilities in each year have been checked by the provincial offices. I think we can conclude here that this is a control system that works. I remember that also from those data which concerned 2018 and 2019 and which were the subject of the information submitted to the High House, this conclusion could also be formulated on this basis. As far as the number of institutions operating without permission is concerned, this question was also raised in the discussion, according to our estimates of such institutions in Poland there may be about 105. In connection with Mrs Chybick's question about leaving social assistance homes of a specialised nature, i.e. those with mental disorders, I would like to draw attention to one thing. I would like to draw your attention to the fact that the bill is primarily aimed at social assistance homes, but also at institutions providing 24-hour care which are not specialised in nature, i.e. those which are not primarily intended for people with mental disorders. As regards the regulation on the issue that Mrs Chybick asked, it is contained in the Law on the Protection of Mental Health. On the other hand, this law actually sets out the terms and conditions under which the possibility of leaving institutions addressed to the elderly, the disabled, in which the care physician found mental disorders can be limited. And here we can say that, on the one hand, we want to ensure that these people are protected by life and health, but on the other hand, we want to guarantee that they have the fundamental value of freedom. As far as questions are concerned about leaving these specialist establishments, and Mr Papke asked, as I say, I do not have this type of data on me, because this question is not directly related to the matter of the law. Mrs Rusecka asked me about the effectiveness and this is a kind of summary of this discussion. It seems to me that, taking into account the statistics on the number of checks but also the number of irregularities and fines found, which have been imposed on establishments operating without authorisation, also taking into account the number of establishments which have been found to have other irregularities, it is confirmed that it is difficult to have reservations about the integrity of this system. I would like to draw attention to the fact that these experiences acquired in the course of the inspections carried out by the voivodships, but also which were the result of what you were talking about, i.e. the practice of those directly carrying out these tasks, from their professional experience, are reflected in the provisions of the proposed law. We are trying to eliminate, address these shortcomings, shortcomings, gaps that have been identified in the practice of applying the provisions of the Social Assistance Act, so as to ensure, on the one hand, the compatibility of social assistance homes, other facilities providing 24-hour care to older people, disabled persons operating in accordance with the law, on the other hand, the legal gaps that some have allowed such activities to be carried out without appropriate authorisation. I think that's it for me. [[Control of the rules. Leaving the house and mandatory rules.]] Yeah, we need to pay attention to two things. The Member asked, you can say, the general principles, i.e. the possibility of introducing... We're not talking about a restriction bill in this draft, we're talking about registration. It is about recording the leaving of a facility by people in such facilities belonging to categories other than those with mental disorders. That's one regulation. We're not talking about a restriction, we're talking about registration. [[These are the rules of leaving.]] No, we're talking about registering leaving this facility, not limiting possibilities. These are two different things. As regards the limitation of the possibility of leaving such an establishment, these are provisions addressed only and only to one category of persons staying in such establishments, i.e. to people with mental disorders, and they, as you must have noticed, are subject to numerous restrictions. It is a doctor's certificate stating the mental disorders and the need to ensure the safety, protection of life and health of such a person, the duration of such a restriction no longer than six months, of course the possibility of appealing on a judicial basis, the possibility of assessing the grounds for the application of this restriction by the Director. He also asked who could introduce such restrictions. These restrictions may be introduced by the director of the DPS - this is the position that, so to speak, legally empowered - as well as by the director of another 24-hour care facility, with a view simply to the well-being, safety, protection of the lives and health of such persons. On the other hand, as far as this first issue is concerned, it is really within the scope of a kind of organizational authority. [[Order orders.]] It's a matter of order, that's exactly what it is. The rules of procedure are those in respect of which such registration of a person staying at a social security home may be introduced. The introduction itself is optional and we are not willing whether we are not able to be persuaded to introduce an obligation of this type of registration. We believe that the head of the facility, the head of the facility, is the right person here to assess the need for such registration. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u406	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending the Law on Minimum Compensation for Work, Parliamentary Printing No. 3599. High Izbo! The aim of the amendment to extend the list of remuneration components to an allowance for traineeships is to ensure a fairer and more transparent shape of the minimum wage for work and to improve the situation of workers receiving the lowest wage. The proposed amendment responds to social expectations. It should be stressed that the proposed change has been welcomed by the employers' and workers' side of the Social Dialogue Council. In addition, the parties argue that excluding the allowance for traineeships from the minimum wage category will contribute inter alia. to increase the motivation of workers and their attachment to the workplace, which is particularly important in the current situation in the labour market. According to the statutory construction, the minimum wage for work is not merely a basic salary. This is the total salary of the employee for the nominal working time of the month in question, so in addition to the basic salary it also includes among others. This appropriation is intended to cover expenditure relating to the purchase, hire, maintenance, maintenance and maintenance of equipment and equipment, including the purchase of equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, equipment, In view of the differences in terms of the name used and the different rules for granting the allowance for traineeships in order to remove possible doubts in the draft law, a definition of the allowance for traineeships is introduced regardless of the name of the allowance adopted by the employer concerned. In the current legal state, a worker with a longer period of employment shall be remunerated at the minimum level of remuneration for work. It may have a lower basic salary than a newly employed person, without an internship or with a short period of employment, performing equal work or work of equal value. In practice, this situation causes dissatisfaction and a sense of injustice among workers with a longer period of employment, whose basic salary is lower than for those just starting a job. This applies to situations where the basic salary of persons who are not entitled to the salary allowance is set at the minimum wage level and is increased annually as a result of the increase in that salary. At the same time, workers with many years of professional experience receive equal remuneration, including both basic salary and salary allowance. In such cases, the allowance in question ceases to be a form of gratification and recognition for the worker as having professional experience in long-term employment. The draft law proposes that amendments should enter into force on 1 January 2020, with the exception of transitional provisions to be applied from 1 September 2019. It is about the possibility for the employer and the employee to negotiate an agreement on an appropriate change of remuneration. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the positive effects of the proposed changes, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the government draft law amending the Law on Minimum Compensation for Work. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u407	Thank you, sir. And I invite Mrs Joanna Frydrych, the Civic Platform Club, the Civic Coalition, to present a position on behalf of this club.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u408	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform - Coalition I am pleased to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending the Act on Minimum Compensation for Work. The purpose of this law is to extend the catalogue of components of remuneration to an allowance for traineeships. This applies to persons who have a longer period of employment and whose remuneration is minimal. The proposed amendment will ensure a fairer and more transparent shape of the minimum wage and improve the situation of workers receiving the lowest wage. Minimum remuneration for work is a guarantee of a minimum level of remuneration for each worker. In the current legal state, the minimum wage for work is not a basic salary. This is the total remuneration of the employee for the nominal working time of the month. The basic salary shall include other components of remuneration and employee benefits included in the staff remuneration, including the salary allowance, so that the staff member is consequently entitled to a minimum salary. It is necessary to emphasise that the allowance for traineeships is not compulsory and the rules for granting this allowance are governed by separate provisions. Depending on the employer's rules, the traineeship entitled to the allowance in question may be of a general, occupational or professional nature. The basis for granting it is an internship, i.e. an objective and verifiable criterion. Taking into account that different employers have different rules for granting an allowance for traineeships and different names are used, which may raise doubts As regards interpretation, this law introduces a definition of the allowance for traineeships. This amendment will result in this allowance being a gratification function and will be an expression of the employee's recognition of his professional experience in long-term employment. In conclusion, only employers who, under separate rules, are required to pay the allowance in question will be excluded from the scope of the minimum wage component. The increase in wages of the least-earned workers with long-term employment may help to stabilise employment, but the proposed change will increase the employment costs of regulated workers and thus may result in lower profits for entrepreneurs. Due to the entry into force of the provisions in question, we can see a slight increase in average wages in the national economy. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Given that the bill will have a positive social impact, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - the Civic Coalition will support this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u437	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present a position regarding the amendment of the Act on Pension Supply to Police Officers, Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Military Counterintelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service, Central Anti-corruption Office, Border Guard, State Security Service, State Fire Department, Customs and Prison Service and their families and some other laws. First of all, I would like to stress that the project is in line with the expectations of the social side. It is the result of separate agreements that were concluded between the social side, trade unions and the government - specifically the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration, the Head of National Tax Administration and the Minister of Justice. The project contains two major changes. The first concerns the pension scheme for uniformed services. It abolishes the requirement of 55 years for retired officers after 25 years of service. It is worth mentioning that the change includes all uniform formations that serve as public security services. It will also include professional soldiers. This novel is undoubtedly a great privilege. It is an expression of respect for and appreciation for the difficult service of these officers - often at risk of health and life. It is also an expression of concern for the smooth functioning of our uniformed services. The second amendment introduced by this amendment concerns the possibility of obtaining a financial equivalent for the time of service, i.e. overtime worked out. It's supposed to be normal in every profession. Unfortunately, so far only employees of the State Fire Department have been able to receive a financial equivalent for over-normative working time of 60%. the value of each overtime. The officers of the other formations, even if those subordinate to the MSWiA, could not count on obtaining a financial equivalent. They could only get free time for overtime, that was normal. Unfortunately, there were also situations where this possibility, i.e. taking time off, was only theoretically available. In practice, for example, police officers carry out so many tasks, that in the case of staff shortages, it is simply impossible to receive overtime at a fixed time. And today this amendment corrects this gross injustice, introduces the possibility of paying a 100% overtime equivalent the value of each non-normative working hour. This will, in our view, introduce greater flexibility in uniform services with regard to the choice of form of equivalent for this extra-normative working time. This novel, as I mentioned at the outset, is a consequence of the commitments made by the government in dialogue with the social side, the commitments that we should support as a Sejm. They concern the legitimate demands of the social side representing all uniformed services in our country. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I declare my support for the government's amendment to the law on pension provision for uniformed officers. Thank you very much.

We have such provisions: In order to ensure the supply of oil and petroleum products to the Republic of Poland in situations of disruption of their supply on the domestic market and to fulfil international obligations, oil and petroleum products stocks, known as intervention stocks, are being created. Intervention stocks include mandatory stocks, mandatory stocks are created by producers and traders, and agency stocks. The agency's stocks are created by the Material Reserve Agency. As mentioned above, intervention stocks are intended to meet oil and fuel needs in specific situations. They are to meet the demand for these products in a quantity corresponding to at least, you can say, 90 days of the average annual transport of daily fuel in a given calendar year. As oil and fuel demand are increasing, this means increasing demand for the capacity of these warehouses so that the relevant reserves are placed on the market, which translates, among other things. problems in obtaining adequate storage capacity for mandatory resources and agency resources. The availability of warehouse space in the Republic of Poland is a condition for the creation of the required intervention stocks, and thus a condition for ensuring fuel safety in Poland, and it could be said that the energy security mentioned by the previous Member. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u552	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I will start by thanking all the clubs for their declaration of support for this bill, because it is indeed an important element of regulation in the energy sector, which has introduced a long-awaited change in the scope, among others. storage areas in the oil system. It is worth recalling that the increase in the legal trading of fuels, which is the result of the fight against the grey zone, is the reason for the legislative measures that have been taken, because no one expected that the fight against the grey zone would result in such a large increase in the legal consumption of diesel and fuel, and thus an increase in oil consumption. Several issues have been addressed in the draft, which have been discussed in detail in the speeches, so I will not repeat what has been presented. On the other hand, to answer questions about the project that have been put, I would like to say that the increase in demand for storage capacity is mainly due to the increase in fuel consumption in our country resulting from normal economic development of the country and, secondly, to the increase in legal consumption resulting from the fight against the grey zone. So it is not that this problem has emerged rapidly and we are solving it at the moment, but only as a result of the long-term action, further action must be taken. With regard to the question of who this is really about, this applies to all market participants, both producers, I mean Orlen, and Lotos, and PERN, i.e. the company transporting oil and petroleum products, storage, as well as the Material Reserve Agency. Recently, as I mentioned earlier in the committee, the President of the Material Reserve Agency has announced tenders for the purchase of additional storage capacity and no storage space was offered, because such storage space was not available. Therefore, the only logical action was to use what is available nominally in an underground warehouse in IKS Solino. Today, these areas are used approximately up to 4.2 million m3 of 6 and by introducing these regulations we create the possibility to use these nominal surfaces virtually fully. The question was what investments had been made in the meantime since 2014. There is a problem with providing precise data, especially for a company that is a listed company. When it comes to providing detailed data, I can do so in a slightly more aggregated way. We can talk about the fact that storage capacity will increase from about 10 million m3 to 13.2 as a result of these activities, and they will be used both on the capacity needed to maintain intervention stocks of agencies and mandatory and will increase the operational capacity for maintaining fuels and products. On the other hand, as far as Pern's investments are concerned, it is foreseen here that between 2019 and 2025 investments will be around PLN 2 billion and will cover investments in the offshore pipeline as well as storage capacity. In Gdansk it will be about 390 thousand. m3, further in the database in Gdańsk twice 100,000, in other places approximately 130 thousand. The fuel has already been given and another 350,000. These will be investments in the next stages in existing bases. With regard to the question of screening, of course, all fuel bases are subject to critical infrastructure regulation, and they are equipped with security, monitoring and security systems, which are open and secret security, so as to have full control over what is happening in the infrastructure itself and around the infrastructure. The project itself regulates the nominal capacity of IKS Solino and certain procedural issues related to the use of these capacities in IKS Solino in a way that has been regulated differently than before in terms of the deadline for oil embossing, leaving the President of the Material Reserve Agency the possibility to assess whether it is necessary and appropriate at the moment to preserve the security of the state of fuel storage in such a way. It is the President of the Material Reserve Agency who controls the state and quality of these stocks and will therefore do so much more objectively and better than if it had been left, for example. market participants. At this point, it is still worth recalling that this issue of storage capacity is extremely important, as we have seen in recent months. I will remind you that from 19 April to the beginning of June, oil contaminated with inorganic chlorides from the territory of the Russian Federation went to Poland and thanks to proper management, firstly flows, secondly, the state and quantity of stocks Poland was completely safe in terms of supply. We lasted 46 days without the oil supply and there were no signs on the market that something was going on in the system. The market and consumers did not feel this situation at all. In other words, the appropriate infrastructure, the proper stocking situation allowed us to manage the situation in such a way that it was not noticeable on the market and on the consumer, and it certainly did not translate into any price fluctuations resulting from this type of operation. Of course, the situation is already stabilizing and normalizing, and thanks to the fact that these storage areas are available and properly managed, it was possible to survive this situation, which was a clear distortion of the oil supply market. I think I've answered all the questions that have been asked. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u558	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on printing no. 3623, i.e. on the draft law amending the Law on investment in the scope of the regasification terminal of liquefied natural gas in Świnoujście. The Law of 24 April 2009 on investments in the regasification terminal of liquefied natural gas in Świnoujście lays down rules for the preparation, implementation and financing of investments in the terminal in Świnoujście and its accompanying investments. For the purposes of the Act, among others, the improvement of the construction process of investments accompanying the terminal, the implementation of which will allow efficient operation or increased use of the LNG terminal. Currently, the catalogue of accompanying investments includes projects carried out by the gas transmission system operator OGP Gaz-System SA, Polskie LNG SA and Polskie Górnictwo Naftowe and Gazownictwo SA. It should be noted that the effective flow of natural gas from the LNG terminal depends not only on transmission networks but also on distribution networks. It is the distribution network that is part of the direct connection between the final customer of natural gas and the LNG terminal. The proposed investments will contribute to the creation of a functional gas distribution system, fully integrated with the transmission system. It should also be noted that these sections of the distribution system with high pressure parameters due to their location and technical parameters complement the key corridors for the supply of gas fuels in Poland. The absence of proposed investment facilitations at the statutory level for these high pressure distribution pipelines may lead to a reduction in the scope, omission or delay during the decision on their construction. The absence of any of these distribution pipelines due to their importance for the efficient flow of natural gas from the LNG terminal will make it impossible to maximise the use of its capacity. Consequently, the failure to adopt the solutions proposed in the draft will reduce the possibility of achieving the overriding objective of the specialization. The proposed solutions are adequate and necessary to achieve the intended objectives. The process of investment processes so far clearly justifies that there are no alternatives to achieving similar effects and therefore the solutions prepared are not excessive in comparison with the objective to be achieved. The project foresees that the implementation of the proposed investment specification will be entrusted to the country's largest operator of the gas distribution system, managing 95% distribution networks across the country, i.e. Polska Spółka Gazownictwa Sp. o.o., and the possibility of using these powers to customers of gaseous fuels which are generators of electricity and heat... Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice intends to support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

But not everyone is treated the same way on the occasion of these investments, there are exceptions. Special provisions apply to areas under the Ministry of National Defence and its units, and give very great privileges in terms of holding back these investments or making changes in their area. The proposed project has not been consulted publicly. Why? The answer is obvious. Citizens would then learn how, contrary to their will, power can limit their property rights according to the principle: power rules, power does not ask. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u580	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! Ladies and gentlemen! The purpose of the Law on the amendment of the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels is due to the need to introduce in the Act of 25 August 2006 on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels provisions relating to the current conditions for achieving the national indicative target. The obligation of a national indicative target, in short: NCW, is one of the elements of achieving the objective of using renewable energy sources in transport, which is linked to the use of biocomponents in liquid fuels and biofuels. The directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 April 2009 on the promotion and use of energy from renewable sources states this. The adoption of the proposed legislation aims to align the levels and ranges of the NCW with the current capabilities of the fuel sector in order to eliminate the risk of a fuel crisis. The solutions proposed in the draft law concern the determination for the following years, firstly, of the national indicative target until 2024, secondly, of the reduction coefficients, and thirdly, of the mandatory blending level for the years 2020 to 2022 on a scale that is feasible for obliged entities, which is crucial in the context of ensuring fuel stability and security for the coming years. The proposed values have been so assessed as to ensure at least the current level of consumption of biocomponents in the coming years, which is important for the national agri-food processing sector, their main producer. Since the use of biocomponents in liquid fuels and liquid biofuels depends directly on the level of consumption of transport fuels in the country, the level of consumption of these fuels depends on the achievement of the national indicative target. The use of biocomponents to compose liquid fuels is currently the most efficient and cheapest method of achieving RES targets in transport, hence mandatory blending. The Act introduces, inter alia, amendments aimed at enabling other renewable fuels and biocomponents to be included in the NCW, modifying the mandatory blending mechanism, including changes notified by KOWR, introducing changes to the functioning of the Low Emission Transport Fund. Many solutions are discussed in detail in the implementing acts. The proposed law is compatible with European Union law. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I recommend that the High House adopt this law. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u596	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Antoni Duda will speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u597	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club regarding the Senate proposal for a law against food waste, prints 2431 and 3655. Food waste is an important environmental, economic and ethical problem. The waste of food occurs at every stage, from manufacturing through processing and distribution, on consumption at the final consumer to final consumption. The scale of food waste at different stages varies. According to the European Commission's 2012 estimates, 89 million tons of food is wasted in the European Union. It's 179 kg per person per year. Most of the food is wasted at the household stage, but the distribution area accounts for about 5% wasted food. In the case of Poland, there are no specific estimates of food waste. Eurostat data in 2006 talked about wasting around 9 million tons a year, as mentioned by the previous speaker. According to data from the Federation of Polish Food Banks, 65 thousand were transferred through food banks in 2015. t food, of which only 1400 t, i.e. approx. 2% It came from donations from commercial networks. In 2016, the amount of food delivered to the banks increased to 92 700 t, of which 4700 t were transferred by distributors. 81 000 are not included in this data. t fruit and vegetables in connection with the Russian embargo. At present, there is no regulation in Poland to prevent food waste. The only convenience in this respect is favourable tax rules. Since January 2009, a donation from producers of food products has been exempt from duty on goods and services, except alcoholic beverages with an alcoholic strength of more than 1,2% and alcoholic beverages consisting of a mixture of beer and non-alcoholic beverages in which an alcoholic strength by volume exceeds 0,5% if they are transferred to a public benefit organisation for charitable purposes carried out by that organisation. On the other hand, as of 1 October 2013, the exemption for donations of the abovementioned food to the public benefit organisation was extended to include its distributors and currently covers all taxpayers making such donations. The proposed law focuses on the distribution sector because the tax facilities in place are mainly used by food producers. Furthermore, the distribution sector, and particularly large shops, with a commercial area of more than 250 m2, has significant potential to reduce food waste, since: firstly, the large scale facilitates the logistics of food transport to food banks, secondly, the advanced technical infrastructure, such as central warehouses, computer tracking systems, allows for a smooth identification of the food for which the shelf life is approaching, while maintaining the continuity of the refrigeration chain, thirdly, the centralised decision-making structure allows for the smooth implementation of procedures to limit food waste. The aim of the proposed changes is to include food distributors in existing and implemented mechanisms to prevent the loss of life. The proposed regulation opens up opportunities for traders selling food in commercial units of more than 250 m2 in which revenues from the sale of foodstuffs account for at least 50% revenue from the sale of all goods, to take action to prevent food waste, creating a mechanism to transfer free of charge unsold food products using existing tax credits in this respect. The proposed law introduces regulations defining the obligation of food sellers to prevent food waste, which aims to reduce the negative social, environmental and economic consequences of waste. It obliges the seller referred to above to conclude a social food transfer agreement with the relevant non-governmental organisations. It also provides for the obligation for food sellers to pay fees for the waste of it. The basis for calculating the fee shall be the weight of the food wasted. The funds derived from these fees will be transferred to the organisations with which the seller has concluded a contract. The project entered the Sejm more than a year ago and was addressed to the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development. The Commission has appointed a subcommittee to consider this project, which in several meetings discussed the project with a representative of the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development. The result of the committee's work is a draft law presented in print no. 3655. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I recommend the adoption of the bill presented by the High Sejm. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u598	Thank you. On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition, Mr Paweł Kobyliński. You're welcome. [[Marshal, I'm handing over the amendments right away. There you go.]] Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u619	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the group of Members, I have the honour to present a draft law amending the electoral code law, print No 3662. The proposed amendment refers to Article. 244 and Article 4 (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [2] shall apply mutatis mutandis. 324 Act of the Election Code of 5 January 2011. The proposed amendment to the electoral code is based on the identification of the competent Chamber, which will recognise in full the electoral protests in elections to the Sejm, the Senate and the European Parliament, the protests concerning the validity of the presidential election by indicating that it will be the Chamber of Extraordinary Control and Public Affairs, and not the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs, which was liquidated by the Supreme Court Act of 8 December 2017. It should be stressed that in accordance with Article. 26 The Supreme Court Act is the jurisdiction of the Chamber of Extraordinary Control and Public Affairs to deal with emergency complaints, to recognise electoral protests and protests against the validity of national referendums and constitutional referendums, and to state the validity of elections and referendums. I would like to stress that the amendment to the article. 324 The electoral code is similar to the amendment contained in Article. 244, i.e. it is an indication of a specific chamber, which will settle in full the protests concerning the validity of the election of the President of the Republic. These amendments aim to maintain consistency between the Supreme Court Act and the electoral code, and at the same time to highlight the importance of the nature of these decisions by the fact that the decisions will be made in full composition, as is apparent from the article. The 77th Supreme Court Act. I therefore ask the High House to adopt this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-17-sejm-84-1.u641	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Yesterday was the 125th anniversary of Henryk Sławik's birth, one of the heroic sons of the Upper Silesian land, who devoted his life to the cause of Poland and Poles. Over the years, this figure has been forgotten and underestimated, which is why I am pleased that today this man is more and more often shown as an example for us and a role model for the next generations. Henryk Sławik was born on 16 July 1895 in Szeroka, today it is part of the town of Hawk-Zdrój. During World War I he fought as many inhabitants of Upper Silesia in the Prussian Army. After returning home he was an active participant of all three Silesian uprisings. In the interwar period he lived in Katowice and actively joined social, local and political life. After the aggression of the Third Reich and the Union of Soviet Socialist Republics against Poland in September 1939, he was in Hungary, where he headed the Civic Committee for the Care of Polish Refugees. Organized assistance for interned military and civilian refugees. He helped to travel to the West in cooperation with a delegate of the Hungarian government, József Antall. He organized documents for refugees, which many of them, including a large group of Jews fleeing Poland, saved their lives. After the German army seized Hungary in 1944, he was arrested. He didn't give up his coworkers in the investigation, he took full responsibility. On 23 August 1944, he was murdered in the German concentration camp Mauthausen. This heroic figure during the PRL period was completely forgotten. It was not until the second decade of the 20th century that attempts were made to restore it to the collective memory of our nation. In 2010, a commemorative plaque was unveiled in Katowice. In 2014, a permanent exhibition was opened in his hometown, Jastrzebie-Zdrój. In 2015 before the building of the International Congress Centre in Katowice, the monument of Henryk Sławik and József Antall was unveiled, while the city council of Katowice gave the square before Spodek in Katowice the name of: Sławik and Antall Square. I am glad that this unique character is now honored and promoted throughout Poland. We need such examples, such heroes, both in times of war and in times of peace, because the attitude of sacrifice and devotion to our homeland and to our neighbors is important at all times and under all circumstances. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

I would like to dedicate my statement to activities that promote the cultural heritage of the historical land of Łomża in connection with the 45th anniversary of the existence of the Łomża Scientific Society to them. Wags. One of the most active associations, among others. promoting the historical heritage of the land of Łomżyńska is Łomżyńskie Scientific Society n.e.i. Wags that celebrate the 45th anniversary of the uprising this year. The aim of the company is to promote science, inspire and conduct scientific research, especially taking into account the historical land of Łomża and the area of north-eastern Poland. An important element of the statutory activity of the Lomżyn Scientific Society is the collection of scientific materials with particular regard to the land of Łomżyn, its historical past, its achievements, as well as cultural and linguistic separation. In recent years, the Lomżyn Scientific Society (Lomżyn Science Society). Wagów has undertaken a research project: Poznan and memory: the historical heritage of Łomża land in its sources (XV-XX century). Identity of persons and places - in the 600th anniversary of Łomża city rights: the Łomża castle (XIV-XVIII c.), the capital of the department (1807-1815) and the governor (1867-1918) financed by the Ministry of Science and Higher Education as part of a very inspiring and helpful research association of the The main idea of the project developers is to save and restore the memory of the former inhabitants of the historical land of Łomża and the Mazowiecko-Podlaskie border, so that the next generations of their descendants have the basis for conscious recognition of their identity and the worthy and effective use, protection and co-creation of the heritage they have acquired. To this end, the interdisciplinary team of the Mazovia and Podlasie Cultural Heritage Research Laboratory, which operates in the Łomża Scientific Society, conducts extensive excerpts of handicraft and printed sources and translates some of them to create a generally accessible monumental documenting base which confirms cultural heritage and shows the identity of the historical land of Łomża and the Mazowiecko-Podlaskie border. through the history of names and their hosts. The aim here is primarily to gather in one place distributed source materials, which would identify people and places in the context of time and various situations and events taking into account the values of the source and the form in which the contents describing people and places are recorded. The result of the project is the website www.name.lomza. en. It contains source materials about the historical land of Łomża, places and persons associated with this area. The materials and sources published on it are among others. a good starting point to search for its roots and identify the inhabitants of Łomża land. As a Member, I support initiatives that promote the culture and heritage of the north-eastern region of Mazovia and its history, which are a contribution to building national identity. Individuals, initiatives and institutions to discover and disseminate the region's heritage should be appreciated and, as a result, their survival and awareness-raising should be built. Such an institution is undoubtedly Łomżyńskie Scientific Society n.e.i. Wags. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
